<td valign="top"><strong>Question:</strong> For a bacterial maltotriose-binding protein that participates in an ABC uptake system, which GO cellular-component term is the best match?<br><br><strong>Choices:</strong><br><br><strong>A.</strong> ATP-binding cassette (ABC) transporter complex, substrate-binding subunit-containing<br><br><strong>B.</strong> Phosphotransferase system complex<br><br><strong>C.</strong> TonB-dependent receptor complex<br><br><strong>D.</strong> Sec protein transport channel complex<br><br><strong>Flash-Next response:</strong><br><br><br><br>1. reasoning: The protein sequence begins with a signal peptide (MRRATYAFALLAILVLGVVASGC&#124;IGGG), indicating it is a periplasmic protein in bacteria — consistent with a substrate-binding protein for ABC transport systems. The question identifies it as a maltotriose-binding protein (MalE) in a bacterial ABC uptake system.<br><br>Evaluating each option:<br>- **A (ABC transporter complex, substrate-binding subunit-containing):** Maltotriose-binding protein (MalE) is the well-characterized substrate-binding component of the maltose ABC transporter (MalEFGK complex). This GO term (GO:0080012) precisely describes the complex to which this protein belongs.<br>- **B (Phosphotransferase system complex):** PTS uses a PEP-dependent mechanism with Enzyme II complexes — a completely different transport mechanism, not involved in maltose uptake.<br>- **C (TonB-dependent receptor complex):** These are outer membrane receptors for large molecules (e.g., siderophore-iron), unrelated to ABC transporters.<br>- **D (Sec protein transport channel complex):** While the protein is exported via Sec, it does not reside in the Sec translocon; it functions at the membrane as part of the ABC system.<br><br>The best match is A, as the protein is explicitly a substrate-binding subunit of an ABC transporter complex.<br><br>2. answer: A</td>

The hydrophobic stretch at the beginning followed by &quot;GC&quot; (a common signal peptide cleavage site) indicates this protein is exported from the cytoplasm.

2. The protein name is a maltotriose-binding protein - these are well-characterized periplasmic binding proteins (PBPs) that serve as substrate-binding components of ABC transporters in bacteria.

3. The function: ABC uptake systems in bacteria consist of:

Adenosine 3&#x27;,5&#x27;-bisphosphate + H₂O → AMP + inorganic phosphate

This makes sense because the CNPase cleaves the 3&#x27;-phosphate from the nucleotide. In the 2&#x27;,3&#x27;-cyclic form, the enzyme cleaves the cyclic phosphate, but when describing it as a bisphosphate (3&#x27;,5&#x27;-bisphosphate), the enzyme removes the 3&#x27;-phosphate to leave the 5&#x27;-phosphate (which is AMP).
